1: ---
2: name: workflow-canon
3: description: >-
4:   A work-design assistant for workflow-server goals, requirements, architecture, contracts and design decisions. Use to author workflow definitions ("write a new activity", "apply this finding"), audit them against the design canon ("audit workflow X", "why is this an anti-pattern?"), review integrations ("check these branches together", "assess integration readiness"), or revise this skill ("update the workflow-canon skill").
5: hooks:
6:   PostToolUse:
7:     - matcher: "Edit|Write|MultiEdit"
8:       hooks:
9:         - type: command
10:           command: "python3 \"${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.claude/skills/workflow-canon/scripts/edit_guard.py\""
11: ---
12: 
13: # Workflow Canon
14: 
15: Workflow Canon is a work-design assistant for shaping workflow-server work around its goals, requirements, architecture and constraints.
16: 
17: - **Work**  Intended outcomes, requirements and constraints.
18: - **Design**  Architecture, responsibilities, definitions and contracts.
19: - **Canon**  The project's authoritative design criteria and conventions.
20: - **Evidence**  Observations supporting design decisions, with their limits stated.
21: 
22: ## Modes
23: 
24: Read the selected mode's file in full, then follow its links as each step needs them:
25: 
26: - **[Author](references/author-mode.md)**
27:   - Authoring definitions and specified changes
28:   - Closing confirmed findings with their prescribed fixes
29:   - Mapped draft walks and verification of written changes
30: - **[Audit](references/audit-mode.md)**
31:   - Canon questions and conformance of existing definitions
32:   - Attribution and verification of findings
33:   - Applicability matrices with independent verification
34:   - Complete coverage reports
35: - **[Review](references/review-mode.md)**
36:   - Scope and revision pairings for integrations
37:   - Tracing design and architecture through declarations and consumers
38:   - Coverage matched to affected branches and languages
39:   - Evidence for findings and readiness
40: - **[Revise](references/revise-mode.md)**
41:   - Changes to this skill's own files
42:   - Conformance with shared and local skill guidelines
43: 
44: ## Rules
45: 
46: - **Measured claims.**
47:   Counts, revision identities and check outcomes come from command output or preserved run evidence.
48: - **One home.**
49:   Project documents own their design criteria. Fetch the authoritative section and apply it as written, including its exclusions; notes taken from it do not replace it.
50: - **Linked sections.**
51:   Retrieve a heading-linked section and its subsections only, stopping before the next heading of equal or higher level. Use targeted search and range reads; follow required prerequisites and expand only to resolve missing context. Explicit whole-document reading requirements still apply.
52: - **Planning.**
53:   When creating or using artifacts, all modes follow [Planning](references/planning.md).
54: - **Commands.**
55:   Before the first command spec, read the [shared command conventions](references/commands.md#conventions) and any additional conventions linked by its caller. Reuse complete prerequisites already read at the same revision; retrieve each operation's spec at its point of use.
56: 
57: ## Dependencies
58: 
59: - **Git and repository search**
60:   Revision capture, comparisons, isolated worktrees and locating criteria and consumers.
61: - **GitHub CLI**
62:   PR metadata, requirements, check evidence and authorized publication through REST on GitHub.
63: - **Project instructions, documentation and runtimes**
64:   Branch ownership, canon homes, schemas and validation tools for the affected product.
65: - **Node and npm**
66:   Engine and corpus checks use the paired engine's runtime, lockfile and installed tools, including TypeScript and `tsx`.
67: - **Docker and Compose**
68:   Packaging checks build and exercise disposable images and containers when deployment behavior is affected.
69: - **Python 3.10+**
70:   The definition edit hook, audit inventory/state/dispatch scripts and their tests.
71: - **Paired YAML parser**
72:   The audit adapter loads the paired engine's installed `yaml` package through Node; its identity is captured with the source snapshot.
73: - **Agent host and sandbox**
74:   Repository access, local execution and artifact writing within the selected mode's authority. Independent sub-agents and observable tool results support audit slices and reading-path validation.
75: - **Claude Code hooks**
76:   Registers the definition edit hook for the session; its script resolves beneath the workspace root named by `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`. Hosts without skill-hook support run the applicable explicit checks.
77: - **Workflow resource access**
78:   A running workflow's resource tool can supply canon sections and report guides when available.

READ 002 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/audit-mode.md | lines 1-52 of 52
1: # Audit Mode
2: 
3: Reviews existing definitions through independently verified change-surface matrices, evidence-backed evaluation, finding confirmation and a complete coverage gate. A single canon question uses the bounded procedure below.
4: 
5: ## Prerequisites
6: 
7: Read the [canon prerequisites](canon-context.md#prerequisites), then its [terms](canon-context.md#terms).
8: 
9: ## Canon Question
10: 
11: For a single question about an entry, locate its [canon home](canon-context.md#homes), [fetch the entry](commands.md#fetch-unit) with its required context, answer and stop. The full audit procedure applies when the request calls for a conformance judgment over definitions.
12: 
13: ## Procedure
14: 
15: 1. **Scope the surface.**
16:    - Read the [criteria context](canon-context.md#criteria).
17:    - Resolve [scope](canon-context.md#scope), then follow the shared [change-surface lifecycle](change-surface.md).
18: 2. **Run the checks first.**
19:    - Select the project's [checks](canon-context.md#checks), preferring a delta run when it can compare the base and candidate under equivalent conditions.
20:    - Capture structural check outcomes now for inventory review; submit their typed observations during the obligations phase after the map is verified. AP/DP evaluation waits for that gate.
21: 3. **Map and verify.**
22:    - Capture the full inventory and four section matrices, decide applicability and independently verify every decision through the shared lifecycle. Resolve all discrepancies before evaluation.
23: 4. **Evaluate.**
24:    - Use [bounded assignments](audit-dispatch.md#phases) for the verified applicable cells and supporting obligations. The coordinator reconciles typed returns and preserves pending work.
25: 5. **Attribute.**  Give each finding its origin per [Attribution](#attribution).
26: 6. **Verify findings.**
27:    - Dispatch independent confirmation through the [obligations phase](audit-dispatch.md#phases). Only confirmed findings drive fixes.
28: 7. **Report.**
29:    - Read [Report Context](reporting.md#report-context) for bands, severity, row fields and coverage.
30:    - Report in the layout [Which Report](reporting.md#which-report) names.
31: 8. **File a mechanised Detect.**
32:    - A Detect applied by pattern is a guard candidate. Name what it keys on and record it against the registry in the report; external filing follows existing user authority.
33:    - The threshold is the second occurrence: twice in one walk, or once in each of two consecutive walks. A `fix` finding counts.
34: 
35: ## Attribution
36: 
37: - **`diff`**
38:   A touched file, or a break that exists because an I/O contract on the change surface changed, including a stale bind or Apply in an untouched referencer or consumer.
39: - **`pre-existing`**
40:   The same construct and evidence at the base ref, independent of an I/O contract change on this surface.
41: - **`known`**  A prior pass accepted this key. Keep the row. Leave it out of the decision surface.
42: - **`fix`**
43:   Text written in this pass to close a finding. Closed within the pass by [Author](author-mode.md), never reported open.
44: 
45: ## Rules
46: 
47: - **Remediation.**
48:   Confirmed findings go to [Author](author-mode.md) when fixing them is within the user's request. A review-only request reports the finding and any criterion correction without editing definitions, canon entries or exemption surfaces.
49: - **Continue.**
50:   Reconcile each return and continue the required residual through [dispatch](audit-dispatch.md#phases). An empty findings list is not coverage evidence.
51: - **Stop.**
52:   Use the shared [completion predicate](change-surface.md#completion). A user stop preserves an incomplete, resumable run.

READ 004 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/canon-context.md | lines 9-16 of 51
9: ## Terms
10: 
11: - **Unit**  One criterion at the level the [canon inventory](canon-map.md#unit-inventory) specifies.
12: - **Entry**
13:   An anti-pattern unit: its Detect finds the defect, its Do not flag excuses a look-alike, and its Fix closes it.
14: - **Walk**  Applying each unit to every file on the surface and recording its status.
15: - **Change surface**  Touched files and their contract closure, resolved through [Scope](#scope).
16: 

READ 005 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/planning.md | lines 1-19 of 19
1: # Planning
2: 
3: A planning record holds the artifacts for one piece of work: requirements, design decisions, analysis, evidence and reports. Modes share its location; the active mode determines which artifacts the work needs.
4: 
5: ## Location
6: 
7: - **Instructions.**
8:   Use the location specified by the user and applicable project instructions. The project default is `.engineering/artifacts/planning/` beneath the workspace checkout that owns the engineering history.
9: - **Resolution.**
10:   Resolve the workspace checkout and planning root before writing artifacts, including when working in a linked worktree. Record the resolved location.
11: 
12: ## Records
13: 
14: - **Naming.**
15:   Use `YYYY-MM-DD-<ref>-<slug>/`, omitting the reference when none exists, unless applicable instructions specify another convention. Derive the reference and descriptive slug from the work; the mode does not impose a folder suffix.
16: - **Continuity.**
17:   Reuse the record already established for the same work. Its artifacts from different modes remain together, with evidence and revision identities beside the conclusions they support.
18: - **Authority.**
19:   Writing, committing and publishing artifacts follow the active mode's authority and the user's instructions. A configured planning location does not grant publication authority.

17: ## Criteria
18: 
19: Use the [canon homes](#homes) at the captured revisions. Read the complete [canon map](canon-map.md) before the first unit for its enumeration and prior-judgment guidance. Fetch each required unit through [Fetch Unit](commands.md#fetch-unit).
20: 
21: Follow covering entries where their Detect reaches, and the owning principle for spellings beyond that Detect. Every walk follows the complete [walk rules](walk-rules.md).
22:

23: ## Scope
24: 
25: Resolve the requested definitions to files, touched paths, changed I/O contracts, their reference closure, consumers and relevant siblings using [Definition Scope](definition-scope.md#scope). Record the base and full revision identities through [Resolve Ref](commands.md#resolve-ref). A new definition includes the files that bind it into the project.
26: 
27: Resolve any prior residual from the shared [planning record](planning.md) by current path before continuing it.

READ 008 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/change-surface.md | lines 1-79 of 79
1: # Change Surface
2: 
3: Author and Audit use the same captured inventory, applicability matrices and evidence gates. Scripts establish structural coverage; independent agents establish semantic accuracy.
4: 
5: ## Capture
6: 
7: 1. **Locate the record.**
8:    Resolve the shared [planning record](planning.md), corpus root and paired engine. Each run has its own directory beneath that record. Read the [audit command conventions](commands.md#audit-command-conventions) before the first audit command.
9: 2. **Declare the scope.**
10:    Fill the [scope input](../templates/audit-scope.json) with paths relative to the corpus root, the exact base commit when comparing changes, and checks selected through [canon context](canon-context.md#checks). Include supporting resources, READMEs, routines, unchanged consumers and relationship context. The guard obligation is required. Candidate draft bytes must exist in an isolated corpus tree before capture.
11: 3. **Capture the bytes.**
12:    [Initialize the surface](commands.md#initialize-audit-surface). It captures source bytes, revisions, parser/tool identities, deleted base files, all AP/DP entries and supplemental canon units. Four Markdown documents contain workflow, technique, routine and activity tables separated by source and section; empty types explicitly say so. The coordinator owns the state and generated views.
13: 4. **Check the proposed inventory.**
14:    Read [Identity](#identity), then assign [independent inventory verification](audit-dispatch.md#phases). Verifiers derive expected items and relationships from captured sources and the corpus manifest. They compare the proposed rows, complete AP/DP inventory, diagnostics and unselected files against those expectations. Automated extraction is a candidate inventory, including unresolved reference candidates; it does not establish completeness.
15: 
16: ## Identity
17: 
18: - **Items.**
19:   Rows use the source path and native section, rule, input, output or step identifier. Nested steps retain their full ancestry and duplicate occurrences. Positional prose, paragraphs and candidate clauses use revision-bound source addresses. Independently testable constraints sharing one line need separate clause rows; verifiers add any missed boundary through the scope input's `rows` list. Whole-section, whole-element, missing-structure and relationship rows retain checks that span items.
20: - **Relationships.**
21:   Each call records its source, target, binding site, executing workflow and complete routine reference chain. Instantiations are separate from routine declarations. Check the paired engine's resolution and inheritance semantics; record ancestor sources and both endpoints explicitly. Add relationships and required context through the scope input when extraction cannot resolve them. An unresolved target can be a measured defect, but uncertainty about its actual target or context leaves inventory verification disputed.
22: - **Supporting material.**
23:   Resource and README rows belong to Workflow / Supporting Material under their actual namespace and source path. A namespace without a workflow definition remains a namespace. Shared content has one content row; referencing elements have relationship rows.
24: - **Criteria.**
25:   AP identity uses the canonical named entry; its current AP number is a display alias. DP aliases retain their numbered source headings and titles. Every row accounts for every captured AP and DP. Fires-on hints can order work; absent metadata or a missing lookup command cannot remove cells. The [canon inventory](canon-map.md#unit-inventory) also supplies family, creation-rule, convention and guard obligations.
26: 
27: ## Applicability
28: 
29: Use [mapping assignments](audit-dispatch.md#phases) to decide every cell in the full row × AP/DP product. Each cell links to source evidence and exactly one disposition:
30: 
31: | Status | Evidence |
32: | --- | --- |
33: | `A` | The criterion's scope and the construct that makes it apply |
34: | `N` | The criterion's excluding wording and source facts satisfying it |
35: | `U` | The evidence or decision still missing |
36: 
37: Shared reasons name the exact assigned row set and hold for every member. A clean result or a Do not flag exception is an evaluation under an applicable criterion; it does not by itself establish inapplicability. Blank cells are unresolved. Table pagination preserves every logical pair exactly once.
38: 
39: After all decisions are `A` or `N`, independent verifiers check every cell, including every exclusion, against the original criterion and source. They cannot verify cells they mapped. The receipt binds the exact mapping reason, disposition and evidence. Disputes return to the coordinator for correction through [reset](commands.md#reset-audit-cells) or [refresh](commands.md#refresh-audit-surface), then fresh verification. Open the evaluation gate only when [status requires the map](commands.md#check-audit-status) successfully.
40: 
41: ## Evaluation
42: 
43: Assign the verified applicable cells through [dispatch](audit-dispatch.md#phases). Workers follow the complete [walk rules](walk-rules.md), read the full source and required relationship context, and return evidence for each pass, finding or blockage. Reconcile returns through the typed [receipt](audit-dispatch.md#returns). Whole-file reads, supplemental units, selected project checks and independent finding confirmation are separate obligations. A command result carries its command, working directory, exit status and captured output; an unmeasured check stays blocked.
44: 
45: The calling mode owns remediation authority. Corrections to definitions return through Author's draft procedure. Review-only audits report findings. Workers have read authority; authorized edits are serialized by the coordinator or assigned disjoint file ownership before execution.
46: 
47: ## Completion
48: 
49: The audit is complete only when the current inventory is independently verified, every AP/DP cell has independently verified applicability, every applicable cell has an evidence-backed pass or finding, and every whole-file read, supplemental unit, required check and finding confirmation is settled. Unknown, disputed, stale, unread, unmeasured or blocked work keeps it incomplete. Every required item has one reconciled primary result; retries cannot overwrite accepted items. Use [status requiring completion](commands.md#check-audit-status) for the final claim.
50: 
51: Coverage completion and conformance are separate. Confirmed violations can have complete coverage; [reporting](reporting.md#report-context) determines their bands and verdict. A user stop or resource limit leaves a saved incomplete run with its residual, never a finished audit.
52: 
53: ## Freshness and Resume
54: 
55: - **Inspect the run.**
56:   Start with [status](commands.md#check-audit-status), which returns bounded counts, generation identity and artifact paths. Locate residuals through the coverage document's headings and assignment identifiers; issue the relevant [packet](commands.md#write-audit-packet) for source, mapping and ownership detail. The script-owned JSON ledger stores the complete dataset and is accessed through these projections.
57: - **Source generations.**
58:   Any corpus membership/byte change, scope correction, canon change, parser/engine change or audit-tool change blocks use of the captured generation. [Refresh](commands.md#refresh-audit-surface) captures the current generation and invalidates all its mapping, verification and evaluation evidence because cross-element dependency reach can exceed local source rows. An identical generation preserves its pending work. Archived state and immutable returns retain prior evidence.
59: - **Mapping corrections.**
60:   [Reset](commands.md#reset-audit-cells) invalidates a selected mapping and its verification/evaluation, including intersecting assignments. A reason or evidence edit changes the mapping fingerprint. Renewed evaluation waits for the entire current map to be verified.
61: - **Interrupted workers.**
62:   Accepted partial work remains recorded. [Retry](commands.md#retry-audit-assignment) retires an assignment and [planning](commands.md#plan-audit-assignments) assigns its unresolved residual. A fresh agent can continue after the coordinator checks captured identities and current status.
63: - **Author byte agreement.**
64:   Before applying a verified draft, compare its captured source hashes with the bytes to write. After writing, capture the actual working tree and run its selected checks. Reuse evidence only for an identical generation; changed roots, contracts or candidate bytes require their current verification. A draft-only request makes no commit-completion claim.
65: 
66: ## Artifacts
67: 
68: | Artifact | Role |
69: | --- | --- |
70: | `audit-state.json` | Coordinator-owned inventory, evidence, assignments and history |
71: | `change-surface-{workflow,technique,routine,activity}.md` | Section matrices, including explicit empty types |
72: | `audit-evidence.md` | Cell reasons, independent receipts and evidence links |
73: | `audit-coverage.md` | Inventory, criterion aliases, assignments and residuals |
74: | `sources/` | Captured source and check-output bytes |
75: | `worker-results/` | Immutable typed returns by assignment and attempt |
76: | `generations/` | Prior state when the captured generation changes |
77: | `audit-report.md` | Generated standalone findings and completion status |
78: 
79: For a workflow-owned report, follow [Workflow Reports](reporting.md#workflow-reports) and transfer the generated counts, residuals and links into that guide's layout. The generated coverage remains authoritative.

READ 009 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/canon-map.md | lines 1-33 of 33
1: # Canon Map
2: 
3: How each home is enumerated, and where a judgement already made is recorded.
4: 
5: ## Unit Inventory
6: 
7: Read the enumeration from the home at the commit audited, with [List units](commands.md#list-units).
8: 
9: | Home | Unit |
10: |------|------|
11: | Anti-Patterns | One `##` family; each `###` entry inside it |
12: | Design Principles | One `##` |
13: | Convention Conformance | One `##` |
14: | Guards | One registry entry in `guards/guards.ts` |
15: 
16: - Read the catalog through its last family. Appended entries share that family, so its title is not the end of the list.
17: - The catalog's first family binds when the change edits a canon home: `anti-patterns.md`, `design-principles.md`, or `convention-conformance.md`. Otherwise it is `not-applicable`, with that reason.
18: 
19: ## Prior Judgments
20: 
21: An entry can fire against an instance a surface has already judged. Read that surface before hand-walking an entry whose registry line claims the check.
22: 
23: | Authority | Where | Records |
24: |-----------|-------|---------|
25: | A guard's exemption surface | In the guard or a module it shares — `EXEMPT_DATA_IDS` in `src/schema/identifiers.ts`; the kind and pipeline sets in `guards/check-prism-lens-reachability.ts` | Instances the check excuses, each with a reason |
26: | Triage ledgers | `ledgers/binding-fidelity-triage.json` and `ledgers/section-framing-triage.json` on the corpus tree | A verdict and a rationale per finding. Binding-fidelity stamps the corpus commit |
27: | Reasoned exemption lists | `walks/option-coverage.json` on the corpus tree | Options a walk need not reach, under the reason that covers them |
28: 
29: `EXEMPT_DATA_IDS` is compiled into the Zod variable schema and the published JSON schemas.
30: 
31: ## File Kinds
32: 
33: Author and Audit establish applicability through the shared [matrix contract](change-surface.md#applicability). The complete [unit inventory](#unit-inventory) supplies the matrix and supplemental obligations. Fires-on metadata, when available, is a prioritization hint.

READ 010 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/walk-rules.md | lines 1-41 of 41
1: # Walk Rules
2: 
3: These govern every walk of the canon's units, in each mode. The criterion applied on a hit is the entry's.
4: 
5: ## Order
6: 
7: - **Entry-major.**
8:   One unit across the surface, paths recorded, then the next. A file-major walk drops later instances of an entry that already fired.
9: - **Compound headings.**
10:   - On a `## Rules` or I/O heading, apply each binding entry to each constraint on its own, before testing the heading whole.
11:   - The entry's Do not flag covers that constraint. A sound first sentence leaves the sibling clause still to test.
12: 
13: ## Evidence
14: 
15: - **The construct.**
16:   A finding quotes or names the construct the entry's Detect keys on. Where that construct is the harness tool surface or a bootstrap resource, read that surface.
17: - **Identifiers.**
18:   Take an identifier at full length from its authority: [Resolve Ref](commands.md#resolve-ref), the registry, the resolved path. The calling mode supplies command prerequisites through its canon context.
19: - **Search hits.**
20:   - A search hit is a site. Read the construct around it, and cite that construct.
21:   - An empty search leaves open a construct the pattern did not name.
22: - **Probes.**
23:   Read a probe's field, label, id, and anchor back from the schema, the registry, or the catalog before reading the result.
24: - **Consumers.**
25:   A claim about what consumes a construct is settled at that consumer: the guard, the loader, or the call sites.
26: - **Forms.**
27:   Where an entry keys on a form, sweep the reference workflows before recording. Convention conformance decides a form the siblings carry.
28: - **Hand-checks.**
29:   - An empty hand-check is `blocked` until it has reported a case already in the corpus.
30:   - Take a set the entry names from that entry's wording.
31: 
32: ## Candidates and Fixes
33: 
34: - **Falling candidates.**
35:   A candidate that falls identifies a correction to the entry's Do not flag or the guard's exemption surface. Apply that correction within authorized remediation; a review-only audit records it for Author.
36: - **Unread paths.**
37:   `unread` paths are outside the remediation scope.
38:   - An audit walks them under [Rules](audit-mode.md#rules).
39: - **Consumers of a Fix.**  Follow the entry's Fix through to the consumer it names.
40: - **Ledgers, fixtures and snapshots.**
41:   Edit the lines a ledger, fixture, or snapshot needs. The diff's size is the check.

READ 011 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/audit-dispatch.md | lines 5-18 of 46
5: ## Phases
6: 
7: | Phase | Required Work | Independent Role |
8: | --- | --- | --- |
9: | `inventory` | Source enumeration, closure, every proposed row and complete canon inventory | Agent other than the inventory author |
10: | `map` | Every row × AP/DP cell | Mapping author recorded per cell |
11: | `verify` | Every settled mapping, including `N` | Agent other than that cell's mapper |
12: | `evaluate` | Every independently verified `A` cell | Audit worker with full source and relationship context |
13: | `obligations` | Whole-file reads, supplemental units, selected checks and finding confirmation | Finding confirmer differs from its author |
14: 
15: Inventory and mapping can proceed together. Verification requires all mapping decisions settled. Evaluation and audit obligations require the complete independently verified map. Plan finding confirmations after findings return. Critical and High findings are re-derived, Mediums spot-confirmed; downgrades state the resulting severity and band.
16: 
17: Use [Plan Audit Assignments](commands.md#plan-audit-assignments), [Bind Audit Agent](commands.md#bind-audit-agent), then [Write Audit Packet](commands.md#write-audit-packet). Bind the actual host agent identity, give it the packet and [return contract](#returns), and dispatch through the host. Scripts do not create agents or turn simulated judgments into semantic validation. Inventory packets carry proposed rows, criteria, diagnostics and corpus manifest slices; captured bytes for unselected sources remain inspectable.
18: 

READ 012 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/audit-dispatch.md | lines 31-46 of 46
31: ## Returns
32: 
33: Return a JSON file following the [receipt example](../templates/audit-receipt.json). The coordinator [submits it](commands.md#submit-audit-results); workers do not edit the canonical ledger.
34: 
35: - **Identity.**
36:   Copy the assignment ID, captured snapshot and host-bound actor. Give each attempt a unique safe name. Return only owned item IDs, once each. Group IDs only when the same source facts and reason hold for every member.
37: - **Evidence.**
38:   Each record has a status, substantive reason and source spans `{source,start,end}`. Mapping, verification and evaluation cite both the row and the actual criterion, plus required context sources. Evidence must overlap the named row and criterion. Absence claims and whole-file reads account for the complete relevant source. Inventory discrepancies name missing rows, clauses, references or criteria; the coordinator corrects scope and refreshes.
39: - **Statuses.**
40:   Mapping uses `A`, `N`, `U`; verification uses `verified`, `disputed`; evaluation uses `pass`, `finding`, `blocked`. Reads use `read` or `blocked`. Supplemental units use `walked`, `not-applicable`, `finding`, `blocked`. Selected checks use `pass`, `finding`, `blocked`. Finding confirmation uses `confirmed`, `downgraded`, `withdrawn`, `blocked`.
41: - **Findings.**
42:   A `finding` record carries a nonempty `findings` array; each member has `band`, `severity`, `origin`, `summary`, `fix`, `entry` and `location`. A cell can expose several violations. Evidence spans sit on the record. Keep entry, location and construct summary consistent across cells detecting the same violation, so the report deduplicates it. Duplicate reports retain their classification; conflicting band, severity or origin requires independent reconciliation. Follow [finding rows](reporting.md#finding-rows) and [attribution](audit-mode.md#attribution). A downgrade also supplies `severity` and `band` on the confirmation record; withdrawal states what did not reproduce.
43: - **Check observations.**
44:   A measured check includes `check: {command: [program, arguments], cwd, exit_code, output}` where `output` names the captured output file. `pass` requires exit 0; a measured finding requires exit 1 and structured attribution. Exit 2 or unavailable execution remains blocked. The coordinator captures the output bytes alongside source evidence.
45: - **Partial work.**
46:   A valid partial return keeps accepted items and leaves the residual incomplete. Failed or invalidated assignments cannot return; a new attempt cannot replace a previously returned item. Correction of an accepted decision uses [reset](commands.md#reset-audit-cells). [Retry](commands.md#retry-audit-assignment) releases residual ownership for a new bounded assignment.

SOURCE {"end": 14, "hash": "0c5b2c9f9813d5f4545fc938f1a0e41b167b3ad6e450db95f35a0f3ef6084025", "source": "corpus/canon/resources/anti-patterns.md", "start": 1, "text": "# Controlled qualification anti-patterns\n\nThese two entries are test criteria, not the project's full canon. Interpret only the binding and instruction constructs defined here. Each distinct failing instruction or binding is one finding; section and whole-element rows can expose the same finding.\n\n## Controlled Qualification\n\n### AP-01. emission-without-validation\n\nDetect: A technique's Protocol instruction or Rules clause explicitly directs an artifact's emission without validating that artifact first. Review each instruction and each semicolon-separated clause independently. Validation elsewhere does not excuse an instruction or clause that explicitly permits unvalidated emission.\n\nDo not flag: A directive to validate before emission satisfies this criterion. A heading, output declaration, retention clause, destination declaration, routine call, binding or graph label does not itself direct emission. Capability descriptions describe the service and are not imperative instructions.\n\nFix: Require validation of the same artifact before emission in every emitting instruction and clause.\n"}
SOURCE {"end": 21, "hash": "0c5b2c9f9813d5f4545fc938f1a0e41b167b3ad6e450db95f35a0f3ef6084025", "source": "corpus/canon/resources/anti-patterns.md", "start": 15, "text": "### AP-02. broken-binding\n\nDetect: Either binding contract fails: (1) an activity's routine invocation has an argument-key set different from the routine's Inputs list; or (2) a routine Outputs binding of the form step-id.output-name names an output absent from the technique called by that step. Resolve the step and technique before comparing their names. The keys below a technique's Outputs heading declare its output names. Check each concrete routine invocation, even when its routine declaration is valid.\n\nDo not flag: Matching argument keys and declared output names satisfy the contract. Argument values such as sealed are opaque test values. The fixture has no optional parameters or aliases. Markdown artifact mentions are not bindings. An output's declared name need not match the name of its exported routine binding.\n\nFix: Match the routine's exact input names at the invocation and bind only outputs that its called technique declares."}

READ 024 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/audit-dispatch.md | lines 31-46 of 46
31: ## Returns
32: 
33: Return a JSON file following the [receipt example](../templates/audit-receipt.json). The coordinator [submits it](commands.md#submit-audit-results); workers do not edit the canonical ledger.
34: 
35: - **Identity.**
36:   Copy the assignment ID, captured snapshot and host-bound actor. Give each attempt a unique safe name. Return only owned item IDs, once each. Group IDs only when the same source facts and reason hold for every member.
37: - **Evidence.**
38:   Each record has a status, substantive reason and source spans `{source,start,end}`. Mapping, verification and evaluation cite every source in each assigned `row.sources` plus `criterion.source`, including exclusions. Evidence must overlap the named row and criterion. Absence claims and whole-file reads account for the complete relevant source. Inventory discrepancies name missing rows, clauses, references or criteria; the coordinator corrects scope and refreshes.
39: - **Statuses.**
40:   Mapping uses `A`, `N`, `U`; verification uses `verified`, `disputed`; evaluation uses `pass`, `finding`, `blocked`. Reads use `read` or `blocked`. Supplemental units use `walked`, `not-applicable`, `finding`, `blocked`. Selected checks use `pass`, `finding`, `blocked`. Finding confirmation uses `confirmed`, `downgraded`, `withdrawn`, `blocked`.
41: - **Findings.**
42:   A `finding` record carries a nonempty `findings` array; each member has `band`, `severity`, `origin`, `summary`, `fix`, `entry` and `location`. A cell can expose several violations. Evidence spans sit on the record. Keep entry, location and construct summary consistent across cells detecting the same violation, so the report deduplicates it. Duplicate reports retain their classification; conflicting band, severity or origin requires independent reconciliation. Follow [finding rows](reporting.md#finding-rows) and [attribution](audit-mode.md#attribution). A downgrade also supplies `severity` and `band` on the confirmation record; withdrawal states what did not reproduce.
43: - **Check observations.**
44:   A measured check includes `check: {command: [program, arguments], cwd, exit_code, output}` where `output` names the captured output file. `pass` requires exit 0; a measured finding requires exit 1 and structured attribution. Exit 2 or unavailable execution remains blocked. The coordinator captures the output bytes alongside source evidence.
45: - **Partial work.**
46:   A valid partial return keeps accepted items and leaves the residual incomplete. Failed or invalidated assignments cannot return; a new attempt cannot replace a previously returned item. Correction of an accepted decision uses [reset](commands.md#reset-audit-cells). [Retry](commands.md#retry-audit-assignment) releases residual ownership for a new bounded assignment.
READ END 2026-10-11T07:15:53.373690+00:00

READ 025 | /home/mike1/projects/dev/workflow-server/.worktrees/skill/canon-audit-matrices/skills/workflow-canon/references/reporting.md | lines 5-76 of 136
5: ## Report Context
6: 
7: Read this section before selecting a report layout.
8: 
9: ### Bands
10: 
11: | Band | Holds | Target |
12: |---|---|---|
13: | **Live** | The definition misbehaves as it stands — a value read before its producer runs, a bind of the wrong value, a gate option that reaches no path | Zero, every pass |
14: | **Contract** | The defect propagates while the run still works — a second home for an owned fact, a constraint with no structural backing, a citation whose home lacks the claim | Zero, every pass |
15: | **Hygiene** | Contained to the prose it sits in | A falling ratchet against the prior pass |
16: 
17: State each band's count. The verdict is Live and Contract. Text a fix writes is checked within its pass, so a pass reports no finding its own fixes made.
18: 
19: ### Severity
20: 
21: Severity ranks urgency inside a band. The band says whether the finding gates.
22: 
23: | Severity | Fires when |
24: |----------|-----------|
25: | `Critical` | Schema-invalid or structurally broken. Must not be committed. Every guard failure is Critical. |
26: | `High` | An entry fires on a construct the change ships, and the defect propagates. Re-derive before it drives a fix |
27: | `Medium` | An entry fires and the defect stays in that construct. Spot-confirm |
28: | `Low` | Hygiene with no consumer consequence |
29: 
30: An unmeasured guard records `blocked` coverage; the [canon check conventions](commands.md#canon-check-conventions) define its exit statuses.
31: 
32: ### Finding Rows
33: 
34: | Column | Carries |
35: |--------|---------|
36: | ID | Stable within the report |
37: | Band | `Live`, `Contract`, or `Hygiene` |
38: | Severity | The scale above |
39: | Entry | As the catalog's entry-identity rule states. A principle by the title its file uses: an anchor that embeds the section ordinal breaks when a principle is inserted ahead of it |
40: | Location | File and field, at the depth the evidence sits |
41: | Evidence | The construct Detect keys on, quoted or named. A closure-only file says which contract it references |
42: | Origin | `diff` or `pre-existing`, per [Attribution](audit-mode.md#attribution) |
43: | Known | Set when a prior pass accepted this key |
44: | Fix | The action the entry prescribes, in one line |
45: 
46: A row whose Evidence cannot name a construct is not a finding. Evidence and Location cite any construct in a change-surface file.
47: 
48: ### Coverage Ledger
49: 
50: The generated [coverage state](change-surface.md#artifacts) accounts for every row–criterion cell, inventory, file read and supplemental obligation. The findings report summarizes divergences and links to that complete ledger.
51: 
52: | Column | Carries |
53: |--------|---------|
54: | Home | The criteria home |
55: | Unit | The section title or anchor, as the home spells it |
56: | Status | `not-applicable` with the unit's own reason, or `blocked` with what prevented the walk |
57: 
58: Derive unknown, unverified, unevaluated and supplemental residuals from current state. Preserve each residual's identifiers. Display counts summarize those lists.
59: 
60: ### File Coverage
61: 
62: Independent of the unit ledger: every unit can be `walked` while the surface stays unopened.
63: 
64: | Disposition | Means |
65: |-------------|-------|
66: | `read` | The whole file was inspected |
67: | `unread` | Anything else |
68: 
69: A scan hit is `read` only when the file was then inspected whole. A scan is evidence for a finding, not a path disposition. `read` and `unread` sum to the enumeration; where they do not, the report states the list that was walked.
70: 
71: List the `unread` paths. While any remain, the header's status is the [coverage header](#coverage-header). An existence claim over a list that still has `unread` paths says so on the finding.
72: 
73: ### Coverage Header
74: 
75: Use `finished` only when the shared [completion predicate](change-surface.md#completion) holds; otherwise use `in progress`. Include the captured generation, row and AP/DP counts, verified applicability, applicable-cell evaluations, unread sources and unresolved supporting obligations. Every count reconciles to the generated state.
76:

35: ## Attribution
36: 
37: - **`diff`**
38:   A touched file, or a break that exists because an I/O contract on the change surface changed, including a stale bind or Apply in an untouched referencer or consumer.
39: - **`pre-existing`**
40:   The same construct and evidence at the base ref, independent of an I/O contract change on this surface.
41: - **`known`**  A prior pass accepted this key. Keep the row. Leave it out of the decision surface.
42: - **`fix`**
43:   Text written in this pass to close a finding. Closed within the pass by [Author](author-mode.md), never reported open.

READ 030 | full assigned criterion bytes and covering introductions | started 2026-10-11T07:17:41.527984+00:00
CRITERION SOURCE corpus/canon/resources/anti-patterns.md hash=0c5b2c9f9813d5f4545fc938f1a0e41b167b3ad6e450db95f35a0f3ef6084025
1: # Controlled qualification anti-patterns
2: 
3: These two entries are test criteria, not the project's full canon. Interpret only the binding and instruction constructs defined here. Each distinct failing instruction or binding is one finding; section and whole-element rows can expose the same finding.
4: 
5: ## Controlled Qualification
6: 
7: ### AP-01. emission-without-validation
8: 
9: Detect: A technique's Protocol instruction or Rules clause explicitly directs an artifact's emission without validating that artifact first. Review each instruction and each semicolon-separated clause independently. Validation elsewhere does not excuse an instruction or clause that explicitly permits unvalidated emission.
10: 
11: Do not flag: A directive to validate before emission satisfies this criterion. A heading, output declaration, retention clause, destination declaration, routine call, binding or graph label does not itself direct emission. Capability descriptions describe the service and are not imperative instructions.
12: 
13: Fix: Require validation of the same artifact before emission in every emitting instruction and clause.
15: ### AP-02. broken-binding
16: 
17: Detect: Either binding contract fails: (1) an activity's routine invocation has an argument-key set different from the routine's Inputs list; or (2) a routine Outputs binding of the form step-id.output-name names an output absent from the technique called by that step. Resolve the step and technique before comparing their names. The keys below a technique's Outputs heading declare its output names. Check each concrete routine invocation, even when its routine declaration is valid.
18: 
19: Do not flag: Matching argument keys and declared output names satisfy the contract. Argument values such as sealed are opaque test values. The fixture has no optional parameters or aliases. Markdown artifact mentions are not bindings. An output's declared name need not match the name of its exported routine binding.
20: 
21: Fix: Match the routine's exact input names at the invocation and bind only outputs that its called technique declares.
CRITERION SOURCE corpus/canon/resources/design-principles.md hash=6c205b2ef7f2ef9498ffe1d801a42438a5350bddac684702a58ea9a525bf2121
1: # Controlled qualification design principles
2: 
3: These two principles apply only to this controlled qualification dialect. They do not stand in for the project's full canon.
4: 
5: ## 1. Honor the executing workflow's inherited destination
6: 
7: A leaf technique's Destination declaration must satisfy the Rules of the ancestor TECHNIQUE.md in its executing workflow's techniques directory. This fixture executes produce through qualification's run activity and deliver routine, so it inherits qualification/techniques/TECHNIQUE.md. Compare the leaf's destination value with the inherited required value. The ancestor's constraint is a declaration, not a conflicting leaf destination. Files without a leaf destination declaration are not independently checked for this principle; explicit call and inheritance relationships must carry the rule to the leaf instance.
9: ## 2. Provide a terminating workflow path
10: 
11: A workflow must have at least one finite path from initialActivity through its graph to a null destination. Each graph key names an activity and each nested label names an exit whose value is the next activity or null. A self-loop is legal only when another reachable path terminates. Assess this as a whole-workflow invariant; matching individual activity IDs does not prove termination. Activity and routine steps do not add graph exits in this dialect.
